# psi-neon-online - Work Plan

## TL;DR (For humans)
<!-- Fill this LAST, after the detailed plan below is written, so it summarizes the REAL plan. -->
<!-- Plain English for a non-engineer: NO file paths, NO todo numbers, NO wave/agent/tool names. -->

**What you'll get:** Mỗi tuần, người dùng mở trang PSI, chọn kỳ, upload bộ file nguồn và bấm Run. Hệ thống kiểm tra, xử lý, tạo Draft để duyệt; khi phát hành Final, dashboard Power BI tự làm mới bằng số liệu đã duyệt.

**Why this approach:** Python/Polars giữ logic PSI đã được kiểm chứng; Neon giữ trạng thái, quyền và dữ liệu phân tích; Google Cloud Storage giữ file vì ổ đĩa Cloud Run là tạm thời; Cloud Run xử lý theo job. Power BI đọc bảng Final-only theo chế độ Import nên dashboard nhanh và không làm lộ Draft.

**What it will NOT do:** Không dùng Supabase, DuckDB, pandas, Spark hoặc AI để tự duyệt/sửa số liệu. Không tái tạo file khi Publish, không cho Power BI đọc Draft/file thô, và không đụng production trước gate phê duyệt riêng.

**Effort:** Large
**Risk:** High - dữ liệu nghiệp vụ, xử lý Excel dài, Draft/Final, phân quyền và Power BI vẫn cần kiểm định kỹ dù luồng upload đã được đơn giản hóa.
**Decisions to sanity-check:** V1 dùng upload trực tiếp trên web, một công ty tenant, Google Cloud Storage cùng GCP và Power BI Import; chia sẻ/tự refresh Power BI đầy đủ vẫn cần license/capacity phù hợp.

Your next move: chọn bắt đầu triển khai hoặc chạy high-accuracy review trước. Full execution detail follows below.

---

> TL;DR (machine): Large/high-risk build delivering direct web upload, deterministic Polars PSI generation, governed Neon/GCS Draft-to-Final workflow, and Final-only Power BI Import refresh.

## Scope
### Must have
- Direct web upload of the weekly source set with explicit period/source assignment, immutable Google Cloud Storage objects, exact source/version provenance, and approved carry-forward selection.
- A deterministic Python 3.13 PSI engine whose business transforms use fastexcel/Calamine, Arrow, typed/lazy Polars, and Parquet; it must reproduce the governed 17-sheet Excel deliverable and Manual Check behavior.
- Neon Postgres is the governance and analytics database and Neon Auth handles invitation-only users. FastAPI is the browser's only control-plane, database, and Auth API; the sole data-plane exception is a short-lived origin-bound browser-to-GCS resumable upload session issued by FastAPI. Neon Data API is not used in v1.
- FastAPI on Cloud Run for the authenticated control plane, one one-task PSI worker Job, one separately privileged one-task backup Job, a private Cloud Tasks queue for durable post-commit delivery, a private recovery broker that alone holds the project-scoped Neon API key, and a production-disabled-until-authorized two-task cutover guardian Job plus private Power BI session/routing brokers and two zone-disjoint private GCE browser executors for independently supervised access-only partial-finalize compensation; all work is started by explicit user, publication, or explicit cutover actions, never a schedule.
- Content-addressed Google Cloud Storage and an append-only Draft -> approval -> Final release protocol whose database/object transitions reconcile after failures.
- Curated Final-only `bi` facts/dimensions, a version-controlled PBIP/TMDL semantic model, five governed Power BI report pages, and publish-triggered Import refresh with observable failure isolation.
- Preview-first IaC, secrets/IAM, migration, backup/restore, retention, cost-capacity controls, weekly pilot, rollback, browser/API/DB/worker/workbook/Power BI QA, and an explicit production cutover gate.

### Must NOT have (guardrails, anti-slop, scope boundaries)
- No Supabase runtime, Supabase migration execution, all-Neon Python rewrite, in-process background jobs, mutable artifact keys, silent latest-file selection, Draft-visible BI rows, publish-time regeneration, or DirectQuery in v1.
- No pandas, DuckDB, Spark, Python row-loop business transforms, authored Rust/TypeScript PSI rewrite, AI approval, AI data mutation, or undocumented KPI invention. Prebuilt native wheels and typed infrastructure/I/O libraries are allowed.
- No OneDrive/SharePoint watcher, Microsoft Graph intake, Cloudflare account, Cloud Scheduler intake, overwrite of user workbooks, `.codex-tmp` promotion, secret/PII/raw workbook evidence, broad staging, production Neon/GCS/GCP/Power BI mutation before cutover, or completion claim without Power BI visual evidence.
- No promise that the end-to-end shared Power BI service remains free: automatic sharing/refresh must pass the actual tenant license/capacity gate.

## Verification strategy
> All technical verification is agent-executed. Owner-supplied business inputs, KPI/license sign-off, and production authorization are explicit external decision gates; when any is absent or invalid, the only valid result is `WAITING_FOR_OWNER` with no downstream or production mutation.
- Test decision: boundary-first TDD with `pytest`, `pytest-asyncio`, Hypothesis where invariants benefit, ephemeral Postgres/Neon preview branches, and HTTP/object/provider fakes; UI and report layout use tests-after plus real browser/Power BI rendering.
- Red/green rule: each engine, schema/RLS, upload finalization, job lease, publish/outbox, and authorization boundary begins with a failing test committed with its implementation; never weaken a failing test or parity fixture.
- Evidence: `<attemptDir>/task-<N>-psi-neon-online.<ext>` where `attemptDir` is the current ULW attempt; outside ULW use `.omo/evidence/psi-neon-online/`. The canonical JSON path `<attemptDir>/task-N-psi-neon-online.json` is reserved exclusively for the no-overwrite aggregate emitted by `build_task_receipt.py`; every task-specific producer uses a distinct semantic filename and may only be consumed as a declared artifact of that aggregate. Precommit local/unit/static checks are development feedback only; every command named in a todo's authoritative QA/evidence sequence runs after that todo's commit from an exact clean commit-bound source context, except Todo 40's explicitly constrained and hash-imported pilot-before-documentation-commit flow. Every evidence index records task/lane, exact commit/tree SHA, sanitized actor/resource IDs, commands and exit codes, checksums, counts, timings, HTTP statuses, schema diffs, traces, screenshots, and any generated sanitized XLSX/ZIP/exported visual data. It must never contain secrets, resumable-session URIs, PII, raw production rows, or owner workbooks; `scripts/validate_evidence_index.py` verifies the schema, hashes, required files, and redaction policy.
- Common gate: `rtk proxy uv sync --frozen && rtk proxy uv run ruff check . && rtk proxy uv run basedpyright && rtk proxy uv run pytest -q`.
- Commit-bound live gate: every container, BrowserOS, Neon, GCS, GCP, Cloud Tasks, Power BI, backup/restore, or other provider mutation/read receipt is executed only after the producing task's source/tests/IaC commit exists and a clean-tree guard binds full Git SHA plus relevant context/plan hashes. A failed post-commit live gate requires a new task-local fix commit and fresh receipt; downstream tasks may consume only the latest passing SHA. Todo 40 is the sole pilot-before-documentation-commit exception and may remain so only because it consumes Todo 39's deployed digest, changes exactly the two allowlisted documentation/evidence-contract paths, and its later commit is proven not to change any Todo 39 tested execution input, including IaC, migrations, cutover/rollback, Power BI, final-gate procedures, dependency/tool locks and wrappers.
- Online gate: preview-only upload, Neon migration/RLS matrix, GCS integrity/fault injection, Cloud Run job crash recovery, BrowserOS neo E2E, Power BI preview refresh/history, and restore rehearsal; local green tests never substitute for these live surfaces.

## Execution strategy
### Parallel execution waves
> Target 5-8 todos per wave. Fewer than 3 (except the final) means you under-split.

- Wave 0 - Authority, executable contracts, and minimal preview bootstrap: Todos 1-5 can run by their declared edges; Todo 6 begins only after Todo 5 freezes retention/CMEK/state policy and owner gates, then installs the checksum-pinned cloud toolchain, creates the durable isolated Neon preview branch/endpoints and owner-gated secret references, and applies only the non-compute GCP preview layer needed by later live checks; it does not deploy application compute.
- Wave 1 - Canonical offline engine: Todo 7 starts as soon as Todos 2-3 finish and does not wait for unrelated Wave 0 contracts; Todos 8-12 then follow their direct matrix edges.
- Wave 2 - Neon, storage, and transactional foundation: Todos 13 and 15 start independently only after Todo 6 has established their durable Neon/GCS preview prerequisites; Todo 14 starts after Todos 4 and 13 and may overlap Todo 15; Todo 16 waits for Todos 13 and 15 and may overlap Todo 14; then Todo 17 and Todo 18 follow their direct matrix edges.
- Wave 3 - Direct upload and durable worker: Todos 19-24, after schema/storage/state-machine foundations. Their Cloud Run/Cloud Tasks client and worker acceptance is provider-independent/local-container acceptance; the first live compute dispatch and queue/dispatcher smoke occurs only after Todo 36 deploys the immutable image.
- Wave 4 - Final promotion plus authenticated workspace: Todos 25-27 and 31 can proceed after Wave 3; Todo 31 commits/applies the KPI mart revision to durable preview and publishes the governed sanitized Final receipt. Todo 28 then binds publish API to local-SQL BI promotion, followed by Todo 29. Todo 30 is deferred until Todo 35 adds BI/security-sync status.
- Wave 5 - Power BI delivery and final workspace certification: Todo 32 starts after Todos 4 and 31; Todo 33 starts only after Todo 32 plus direct prerequisites Todos 4, 6, 13-14, 17, 24, 28, and 31, and consumes Todo 31's successor schema/Final. Todo 35 extends Todo 29 with publication and membership-security refresh; Todo 30 then certifies the complete browser surface; Todo 34 follows its direct edges.
- Wave 6 - Deployment, operations, preview E2E, and pilot: Todos 36-40. Todo 36 waits for the Power BI refresh handler, commits before clean build/deploy, and provisions/tests the production-disabled two-task cutover guardian, private Power BI session/routing brokers, and two zone-disjoint private browser executors in preview. Todo 38 commits backup before rebuild/rebind; Todo 37 then commits observability and again rebuilds/rebinds the complete compensation-aware workload set before live telemetry; Todo 39 commits orchestration before the final clean rebuild/rebind/E2E. No production mutation occurs.
- Release-candidate assembly checkpoint - after Todo 40 pilot evidence passes, commit Todo 40; with all Todos 1-40 now committed, freeze the resulting Git commit as the release-candidate SHA and atomically build the Todo 1-40 evidence index. No source commit occurs between that freeze and F1-F4.
- Release-candidate review wave - F1-F4 run in parallel on that same immutable preview release-candidate SHA/base index and must all approve.
- Post-review cutover - Todo 41 is the only production-mutation step and starts only after F1-F4 plus owner authorization; it provisions and provider-verifies the locked-retention foundation, validates backup or an explicit empty-deployment waiver, migrates/deploys the complete compensation workload set with consumer access disabled, performs QA-only Power BI/canary, publishes a generation/hash-bound guardian bundle, live-proves both guardian tasks/brokers/browser-executor zones before rollback-ready, and lets `finalize` grant consumer access only while that chain remains armed and enable public routing only after candidate-bound terminal membership/session validation. Partial-finalize compensation is access-only: exact group removal, public-route disablement, per-member denial/signout, and private retained deployment; full restoration remains the separate incident path.

- [ ] 4. Approve the Power BI semantic and licensing contract before report code
  What to do / Must NOT do: Create `powerbi/kpi-contract.yaml`, `powerbi/refresh-budget.yaml`, and a source-control contract for PBIP/TMDL. Freeze grains, columns, relationships, measures, formats, filters, refresh policy, and five pages: Overview/Release Health, Revenue vs Target, Inventory, Pre-orders/Purchase, Mismatch/Data Quality. V1 selects a dedicated Power BI Pro workspace with Pro licenses for every publisher/viewer; pinned PBIP/TMDL source with explicit acceptance of its preview status; a separately specified Python 3.12 `fabric-cicd` deploy environment; an owner-provided Windows runner with a pinned Power BI Desktop build and the preview Desktop Bridge CLI enabled; a dedicated Entra service principal with Contributor deployment scope; one approved consumer Entra security group; and a separate read-only Neon `bi_reader` credential plus the named workspace/Entra admin authorized to perform first binding, semantic-model RLS membership, QA-only app publication/audience setup, and user-lifecycle suspension/reinstatement through the Power BI portal. Because the selected Pro workspace is shared capacity, freeze an exact per-semantic-model rolling provider-day budget of at most eight API/scheduled refresh requests: one routine weekly publication, one rollback, four coalesced security-refresh windows, and two reserved failure/incident retries. Publication/rollback/security intents are durably prioritized; security changes coalesce to the highest monotonic membership version per window while every affected user stays `PENDING_SECURITY_SYNC`; retry never consumes another category's reservation without a separate owner incident receipt. The ledger records provider-observed request count, tenant/provider quota timezone and next reset, reservation class and deadline before enqueue. If no reserved slot can meet the security or release SLO, the action remains fail-closed `WAITING_FOR_CAPACITY`; owner must approve PPU/Premium/Fabric capacity before relaxing the eight-request contract. Enhanced refresh is forbidden on shared capacity. The Power BI Groups API is the only automated consumer-access mutation surface and is used solely to add/remove workspace Viewer; the Apps API is read-only inventory, app publication/audience mutation is owner-portal-only, and XMLA is never used to write RLS memberships. Todo 4 records only the binding owner/procedure, credential-secret owner, consumer-group opaque ID/owner, two QA user identities with disjoint team memberships, one admin test identity, the tenant Execute Queries setting owner, the post-deployment RLS-role/temporary-Build grant procedure, and the owner procedure for opaque proof that both QA Viewer identities are active members of the approved consumer group before refresh/query QA. Every access grant/revocation enters `PENDING_ACCESS_PROPAGATION`; Groups/API inventory is evidence of requested membership only, never effective access. For each affected delegated user, the owner procedure calls Refresh User Permissions at most once per user per hour when applicable, waits at least two minutes, discards old tokens/cookies, and uses an isolated fresh sign-in for positive and negative Service checks. Incident removal additionally requires owner-controlled Power BI/Entra session revocation or sign-out and fresh-sign-in denial; public traffic and lifecycle reinstatement stay blocked until effective access is proven. The fail-closed lifecycle procedure inventories and revokes every affected principal's consumer-group membership plus any direct workspace Viewer, semantic-model RLS, temporary Build grant, and effective QA-app access/group membership before an app membership/team/deactivation change can become effective; after the imported security bridge refreshes and is verified, an active user may be reinstated only through the approved group/RLS path, while a deactivated user remains removed. It explicitly does not require model-scoped receipts before Todo 33 creates the model. Select dynamic model RLS: the named admin assigns the approved consumer group to semantic-model `PSIViewer` through the supported portal before activation, while the supported Groups API independently controls workspace Viewer; `PSIViewer` filters a security-membership bridge by normalized Entra UPN via `USERPRINCIPALNAME()`, relationships restrict every fact to allowed tenant/team rows, and only named authoring identities bypass consumer RLS. Actual UPNs stay outside evidence. Todo 33 receipts portal-owned model/RLS and QA-only app setup, Groups-API workspace membership, both QA group memberships, and temporary QA Build grants. The approved consumer group is never placed in an app audience; production access is activated only by adding workspace Viewer. Every KPI must cite a governed PSI field/formula. Enforce the one-cycle RPO operationally: after one Final is committed, the next Final is blocked and new Runs pause before the threshold until the preceding Final's logical dump, governed object copies, and recovery branch all validate; only a separately authorized, expiring, audited two-person emergency override may proceed and it creates a blocking incident. Add a distinct mutable `terraform_state` recovery class: GCS Object Versioning makes every superseded state generation recoverable for at least 90 days, without Bucket Lock on the current mutable state object; a lifecycle rule may make a generation deletion-eligible on day 90, explicitly configured soft delete can extend actual recoverability, and the mapped state CMEK version must remain usable until provider readback proves that generation irrecoverable after lifecycle deletion and soft-delete expiry. Evidence stores hashes and provider metadata, never state bytes; a detected fallback is an incident and cannot be called zero-local-state before reconciliation. Never silently delete protected artifacts or retained state generations, disable/destroy a referenced key version, force-push or discard fallback state, or market the service as indefinitely free.

- [ ] 17. Implement job, Draft, publish, rollback, and outbox state machines
  What to do / Must NOT do: Encode legal upload/run/job/Draft/approval/release/publication-intent/pointer-event/Power BI refresh/backup/security-refresh transitions, canonical idempotency keys, fenced leases, exact role separation, content verification, append-only release pointers, exact-Draft publication, local-SQL BI promotion, and transactional outbox. First reserve a persistent `publication_intent` in a short DB transaction with a deterministic ID/unique tuple over tenant, action kind, approved Draft or rollback target release, expected current-pointer version, and client idempotency key. A retried/uncertain request must resume that same intent; a later intentional rollback after the pointer version changes gets a distinct intent even if it targets the same old release. For each refresh/backup/security delivery persist `(intent_id, channel, delivery_generation, task_name, queue_policy_version, queue_policy_sha256, policy_max_attempts, policy_max_retry_duration, provider_first_dispatch_at_nullable, provider_dispatch_count_nullable, provider_observed_at_nullable, computed_deadline_nullable, state)`; only the queue policy fields are copied from Todo 6's live `GetQueue` receipt. The provider dispatch fields start null and are updated append-only only from the dedicated reconciler identity's authenticated `GetTask(name, responseView=FULL)` snapshots of `firstAttempt.dispatchTime`/`dispatchCount` while the task exists; ordinary `BASIC` responses and handler headers cannot populate them. The deadline is computed only from observed first dispatch plus the persisted max retry duration, never invented from queue creation or handler time. For a security delivery, the intent key includes actor, old/new membership-version checksum, and Entra suspension receipt; legal states are `PENDING_SECURITY_SYNC` -> `ACCESS_SUSPENDED` -> `REFRESHING` -> `VERIFIED`, followed by owner-receipted `REINSTATED` only for active users, while deactivation terminates at `REMOVED`. Generation 0 task names are deterministic from intent/channel/generation, and an uncertain create retries that exact name. Before any later Final, locally require the preceding current Final's dump, governed-object copies, and recovery branch to be `VALIDATED`, unless there is no preceding Final or a separate unexpired two-person emergency-override record from Todo 5; otherwise set `BACKUP_BLOCKING_NEXT_FINAL`, pause new Runs, and perform no GCS/task/pointer work. Before the Final transaction, use Todo 15's publication identity to copy the exact approved Draft generation to a deterministic content-addressed Final key with destination `ifGenerationMatch=0`, then verify destination generation/size/CRC32C/SHA-256 and persist that candidate on the intent. If create returns timeout/connection loss/`412`, read only that exact Final key and adopt it only when generation metadata and full checksum match the intent; a mismatch is `WAITING_FOR_OPERATOR`. Then create the generation-0 private Cloud Task scheduled after the commit window and run the Final transaction: lock the intent, verify only the locally persisted staged and Final-object candidate hashes, promote BI rows, append the Final release with exact object key/generation/checksum plus current pointer/audit/outbox deliveries, mark the intent committed, and commit. Task creation failure blocks Final but the deterministic retained candidate is safely adopted on retry. The authenticated dispatcher validates task/body generation, records each delivered attempt/header as supplemental evidence, acknowledges success/permanent contract failure, and returns a retryable response for retryable work; handler headers alone never prove provider dispatch count or exhaustion. Only Todo 24 through the dedicated full-view reconciler may mark provider exhaustion from persisted provider observations plus live absence. Current Cloud Tasks v2 `RetryConfig` documentation states that retry stops only after both `maxAttempts` and `maxRetryDuration` are satisfied; therefore exhaustion requires no success, matching policy, monotonic authoritative FULL observations, repeated `NOT_FOUND`, observed dispatch count at the attempts limit, and a first-dispatch-derived retry-duration deadline elapsed. Attempts-only or duration-only evidence remains `RETRYABLE`; missing/regressing evidence remains `WAITING_FOR_OPERATOR` (`https://docs.cloud.google.com/tasks/docs/reference/rest/v2/projects.locations.queues`). API/worker/dispatcher/backup/broker cannot use full view; runtime identities may create/get ordinary tasks as explicitly required but have no queue purge, task delete, or task run permission. Authorized replay increments `delivery_generation`, preserves history, and creates a new generation-suffixed task name. Application state—not a nonexistent Cloud Tasks DLQ—owns exhaustion/manual replay. Never perform remote I/O inside the Final transaction, point a release at the Draft key, use per-task retry configuration/Preview API fields, or dedupe by release ID alone.

- [ ] 19. Implement the direct weekly upload contract and limits
  Authoritative Round 20 GCS endpoint/timing replacement: every resumable initiation uses only the XML API endpoint `https://storage.googleapis.com/<bucket>/<object>` with the exact approved `Origin`; FastAPI validates the returned `https://storage.googleapis.com/` session URI and bucket/object binding, and the browser sends that same `Origin` on every upload/status request. The JSON API endpoint is forbidden because its CORS behavior is not origin-restrictive; missing, wrong, or changed Origin fails. Server cancellation uses `DELETE` against that exact XML session URI with the bound Origin and treats only the documented endpoint response/provider-expiry readback as terminal. All scheduled-expiry, immediate-revocation, decrypt, `DELETE`, timeout, `CANCELLATION_PENDING`, ciphertext-destruction, and provider-expiry checks in Todo 19 are provider-faithful local-fake contract tests only and make no deployed-callback claim; Todo 39 is the sole mandatory deployed canary after Todo 36 provides callable API/dispatcher compute. This replacement supersedes every shorter endpoint or live-smoke implication below. References: `https://docs.cloud.google.com/storage/docs/cross-origin`; `https://docs.cloud.google.com/storage/docs/performing-resumable-uploads`.
  What to do / Must NOT do: Define one upload form/session for `YYYY-Www`, source slots, allowed XLSX types/sizes, resumable GCS upload intents, checksum/finalize, replacement versions, expiry, authorization, and clear validation errors. Freeze the one allowed upload origin set; FastAPI creates each GCS resumable session server-side for that exact origin and temp object, returns its bearer URI only in a no-store response, sets narrow GCS bucket CORS for that origin/method/header set and bounded response headers, and ships CSP/connect-src permitting only the exact FastAPI and GCS upload endpoints. The application authorization TTL is short and server-recorded; the bearer URI is volatile-memory-only in the browser and exists server-side solely as the authenticated envelope-encrypted cancellation capability, redacted from logs/traces/errors/analytics/referrers, never stored in HTML/DOM persistence/localStorage/service-worker caches/history/evidence, and is immediately denied by the application after expiry, cancellation, finalization, or team/session revocation while exact-URI provider cancellation is verified or remains explicitly `CANCELLATION_PENDING`. The user experience is simply select files, upload, and Run; do not expose bucket concepts or create watchers.
  Authoritative session-lifecycle replacement: “short-lived” means the application authorization TTL, not a shortened provider-native lifetime. Before GCS initiation, FastAPI atomically persists a deterministic `PREPARED` upload/cancellation intent with tenant/team/temp-object/application-expiry but no URI. After GCS returns the session URI, FastAPI envelope-encrypts it into that row, commits a deterministic expiry-cancellation task intent, creates/adopts the exact-name private Cloud Task with `scheduleTime` equal to the application expiry, and only then returns plaintext once in the no-store response; the browser keeps it in volatile memory only. If initiation response is lost before the URI can be encrypted, the browser receives nothing, the intent becomes `ORPHANED_UNCERTAIN_INITIATION`, finalize/Run stay denied, and the deterministic temp key remains quarantined through provider expiry/lifecycle cleanup because cancellation without the bearer URI is impossible; the plan never fabricates or retries that URI. Deterministic Cloud Task creation uncertainty is reconciled by exact task name, and membership/team/session revocation advances the cancellation-intent generation and enqueues an immediate task. Expiry, explicit cancellation, finalization, membership/team change, deactivation, or application-session revocation immediately denies finalize/Run, enqueues the exact session ID for server-side `DELETE`, and quarantines the bound temp object. The cancellation handler decrypts only inside the narrow API boundary, sends `DELETE` to the exact URI, reconciles timeout/partial response without creating a new session, and retains ciphertext only until cancellation or provider expiry is independently confirmed; then it atomically destroys the ciphertext and cleans the temp generation. A cancellation failure is `CANCELLATION_PENDING`, never “invalidated”: upload bytes may still reach the provider capability, but no snapshot/finalize/Run is accepted, retry continues, and the orphan is inventory-bound until cancellation/provider expiry. Provider documentation's normally one-week resumable-session validity is the outer bound, not the product TTL.

  References: `docs/PSI_PROCESS_UPTODATE.md:3-18`; `docs/PSI_SHARED_TOOL_PLAN.md:36-54,66-82`; GCS contract Todo 15; resumable upload/cancellation lifecycle `https://docs.cloud.google.com/storage/docs/performing-resumable-uploads`

- [ ] 24. Reconcile retries, orphan objects, stale leases, and exhausted work
  What to do / Must NOT do: Add bounded whole-attempt and stable queue-level Cloud Tasks delivery policies, stale lease recovery with monotonic fencing, cancellation, Postgres-owned `RETRYABLE`/`EXHAUSTED`/`WAITING_FOR_OPERATOR` states, alerts and generation-based admin replay, orphan intent/task/temp/staging cleanup, Final-candidate/object/DB consistency scan, and outbox reconciliation for worker, publication, refresh, backup, and security-refresh events. Reconciliation maintains `BACKUP_REQUIRED`/`BACKUP_BLOCKING_NEXT_FINAL` until the exact preceding Final's dump, governed-object copies, and recovery branch are all `BACKUP_VALIDATED`; it pauses new Runs/next Final before the one-cycle threshold, and an emergency override creates an incident without clearing backup debt. For an uncommitted deterministic Final candidate, reconcile only against its persisted publication intent: exact key/generation/checksum match remains adoptable for the next retry; missing object remains retryable before creation; mismatch or an object without one exact live/committed intent becomes `WAITING_FOR_OPERATOR` and is never silently deleted from the locked bucket. Run the delivery reconciler without a schedule by having authorized status reads, new publication/admin actions, and the explicit operator command invoke Todo 36's private one-task delivery-reconciler Job; the caller receives only narrow Job run permission and cannot impersonate or read credentials of the Job service account. It assumes only Todo 6's dedicated queue-scoped reconciler identity; before evaluating a nonterminal delivery, call `GetQueue` and require normalized retry/rate configuration to match the persisted `queue_policy_sha256`, then call `GetTask` by generation-specific name with `responseView=FULL`. Drift, a `BASIC`/redacted task response, or inability to prove the dedicated reconciler identity is `WAITING_FOR_OPERATOR`. While the task exists, persist monotonic snapshots of `firstAttempt.dispatchTime` and `dispatchCount`, compute deadline only from the first field, and reject provider counter/time regressions. Mark `EXHAUSTED` only when all facts hold atomically: no recorded success, live policy still matches, monotonic authoritative full-view provider snapshots exist, repeated full-view `GetTask` is `NOT_FOUND`, the observed dispatch count reached the persisted `max_attempts` limit, and the provider-observed first-dispatch-derived `max_retry_duration` deadline elapsed. This conjunctive predicate matches the current Cloud Tasks v2 `RetryConfig` contract. If either bound is unmet, the duration deadline is future, task is live, lookup is ambiguous, or the retained name returns `ALREADY_EXISTS`, keep `RETRYABLE` and recheck. A vanished task with no authoritative observation proving both provider bounds, a provider/admin deletion before both bounds are proven, or non-monotonic provider evidence becomes `WAITING_FOR_OPERATOR`, never `EXHAUSTED` or auto-recreated. Exact-name `CreateTask` retry is permitted only for a persisted `CREATE_UNCERTAIN` delivery with no prior confirmed-create/provider observation; `ALREADY_EXISTS` remains ambiguous/recheck. Replay increments the generation under a unique admin idempotency key and creates a fresh task name. Preserve exact source set and previous attempts; never use API/worker/dispatcher credentials for full-view reconciliation, claim a native DLQ, grant runtime delete/purge/run, use per-task retry fields, blindly retry permanent failures, reuse an exhausted name, or synthesize a delivery without its persistent intent/event.

  Acceptance criteria: typed failures classify retryable/permanent/ambiguous; only the dedicated reconciler's matching `GetQueue` policy + monotonic `GetTask(responseView=FULL)` evidence + repeated `NOT_FOUND` + both the provider-observed attempt-limit and provider-first-dispatch-derived elapsed-duration bounds can record `EXHAUSTED`, with generation/observations/error/deadline/action and one alert. Attempts-only/duration-only/pre-deadline/live-task/retained-name cases remain `RETRYABLE`; policy drift, BASIC/redacted responses, vanished never-observed/deleted tasks, and counter regression become `WAITING_FOR_OPERATOR`. Final-candidate reconciliation adopts only an exact intent/key/generation/checksum match, never deletes a locked orphan, and alerts on unowned/mismatched objects.   Authoritative Round 29 independently hosted Power BI session execution: `psi-powerbi-session-broker` is a private Cloud Run authorization/router only; it does not claim that Secret Manager or the Power BI Apps REST API can create interactive sessions or mutate app audiences/RLS. The two zone-disjoint `psi-powerbi-session-executor` GCE instances each run the reviewed Chromium executor behind private internal HTTPS, verify a Google-signed OIDC token for an exact executor-specific audience plus mTLS, have no public IP, and use separate CMEK-encrypted persistent profile disks. Before any production grant, the owner connects through IAP, completes MFA separately in named opaque admin and two direct-member browser-profile slots on both instances, and signs an expiring authority receipt that binds release/attempt, executor instance/disk/profile generations, exact workspace/group/subject set, allowed workflows `remove-workspace-viewer,readback-workspace-access,refresh-current-user-permissions,fresh-isolated-member-denial,signout`, and expiry at or after the compensation deadline; passwords, MFA seeds, tokens, cookies, UPNs, and profile bytes never enter GCS, Neon, Secret Manager, logs, or evidence. Secret Manager stores only exact-version profile-wrapping keys and broker-to-executor mTLS material; only each executor identity reads its own versions. The broker may invoke only those two endpoints and route a frozen workflow to the alternate zone on health/fence failure; it cannot publish/update Power BI apps, change app audience/RLS/binding, or read session material. A live pre-grant canary runs every allowed read/session action in every primary/failover admin/member slot, proves no MFA/consent prompt, records redacted provider request/readback and signout receipts, and blocks grant if either executor, any required slot, authority, or key version cannot remain valid through the compensation deadline. Terminal cleanup signs out every slot, revokes the broker operation, destroys the ephemeral profile-disk generations, and independently proves both executors can no longer use the session authority. Automatic partial-finalize compensation is therefore narrowed to supported host-independent actions only: remove the exact consumer group Viewer grant, disable public routing, read both back, refresh permissions as each exact member in isolated executor slots, prove effective denial, and sign out. It never claims automatic app/audience/RLS/binding restoration; any QA access removed during the failed attempt stays removed, the already reviewed pre-finalize deployment remains private, and a later retry requires a new owner-authorized attempt. Full image/database/Auth/object/final-pointer/Power BI/DNS/empty-foundation restoration remains solely the separate post-finalize incident rollback path with fresh owner authority, not the guardian safe terminal.

- [ ] 38. Implement backup, locked retention, restore, and disaster-recovery rehearsal
  Authoritative Round 22 recovery-target Auth state classification: before creating any restore-target Auth intent, `configure_neon_restore_target_auth.py inspect` performs the safe Auth-details GET and writes `<attemptDir>/task-38-target-auth-prestate.json` for the exact authorized recovery target. `ACTIVE` is accepted only when project/branch/database/provider/schema/config/JWKS/trusted domains and the empty-identity/data plus allowlisted-bootstrap fingerprints match; it is adopted GET-only with zero intent and zero POST. `ABSENT_EMPTY` requires provider-confirmed absence plus the independently empty target fingerprint, then and only then persists the enable intent and sends one at-most-once POST before matching GET reconciliation. The configure command consumes `--auth-prestate <attemptDir>/task-38-target-auth-prestate.json --allow-auth-state ACTIVE,ABSENT_EMPTY --active-get-only-zero-intent-post --absent-empty-persist-intent-at-most-once-post`; all other states are `WAITING_FOR_OPERATOR` before Auth/database mutation, and QA proves both branches plus zero POST/intent in `ACTIVE`.
  What to do / Must NOT do: Consume the publication-event backup delivery from the private Cloud Tasks dispatcher to invoke the separate backup Job after every new Final; also support guarded pre-migration and explicit operator backups. A committed Final atomically enters `BACKUP_REQUIRED`; only validation of its exact logical dump, governed-object copies, and recovery branch changes it to `BACKUP_VALIDATED` and unblocks the next Final/new Runs, while an expiring two-person emergency override remains an audited incident rather than backup success. Implement the backup entrypoint before enabling its Job. The Job has its own service account and secrets. It first asks the broker to create/adopt and freeze the exact recovery branch. Only after verified branch identity/fingerprint does the broker create or rotate a child-branch-only administrator credential, use it inside the broker boundary to create a short-lived SELECT-only backup-export role with `BYPASSRLS`, and return only that export credential to the backup Job. The backup Job never receives the child administrator credential; every credential it can read is independently denied against the primary. Before the first export, a commit/toolchain-bound capability gate records the frozen child branch's `server_version_num`, requires the selected checksummed `psql`/`pg_dump`/`pg_dumpall`/`pg_restore` major to equal the server major, creates and verifies the exact export-role shape on that child only, proves `BYPASSRLS` plus `NOSUPERUSER NOCREATEROLE NOCREATEDB NOREPLICATION` and no `neon_superuser`/administrator membership, then revokes the role and destroys the capability-test child administrator. A version mismatch or inability to construct that exact role is `WAITING_FOR_OWNER` before dump or protected-object mutation. The live export uses a newly rotated child administrator and the same verified construction. The export role is revoked immediately after export and the broker destroys the child-only administrator credential. Its storage source role uses a provider IAM custom role containing only `storage.objects.get`, bound under Todo 6 UBLA with policy version 3 and exact `resource.name.startsWith(...)` conditions for committed immutable `raw`/Parquet/Draft/Final/validation prefixes, with no bucket/object listing and no write/delete. Inherited project/bucket/managed-folder bindings are audited so effective access remains no broader. Provider IAM is prefix-scoped only; the application must independently require every exact bucket/key/generation/SHA-256 from the signed database manifest, issue generation-match reads/copies, and reject any unmanifested or generation-drifted object. A destination role containing only condition-bound `storage.objects.create` and `storage.objects.get` is scoped to the exact protected backup prefix under policy version 3; it allows the backup Job to create and then read back only deterministic backup keys, while list/overwrite/delete remain denied. Every dump/copy uses destination `ifGenerationMatch=0`; after timeout/connection loss/`412`, the Job reads only the exact deterministic destination key and adopts it only when generation/size/CRC32C/SHA-256/CMEK metadata match the persisted operation, otherwise `WAITING_FOR_OPERATOR`. Require Todo 6's provider-readback retention-lock receipt before the first protected write. It has no Neon API key: it submits only typed branch-operation requests to the private Todo 36 recovery broker, which owns the project-scoped key and enforces the approved project/source/prefix/time-window/method policy. It cannot read temp/unfinalized uploads, enumerate unrelated prefixes, call unsupported broker operations, or run the PSI worker, while the PSI worker cannot read backup credentials/objects. Create/adopt the broker-protected Neon recovery branch first from the exact post-Final/pre-migration parent LSN/timestamp and freeze writes on that branch; the branch is the complete-database/Auth recovery source because all Managed Auth state branches in `neon_auth`. On that exact branch, hold one read-only `REPEATABLE READ` coordinator transaction open, call `pg_export_snapshot()`, and require both the signed database/object manifest queries and `pg_dump --snapshot=<exported-snapshot>` to use the same branch ID, parent LSN, and exported snapshot; the branch-only backup role must include every governed RLS table and required `neon_auth` class, then be revoked and independently denied before success. In the same snapshot envelope, create a sanitized allowlisted password-free global-role topology and membership manifest (`pg_dumpall --roles-only --no-role-passwords` filtered against the committed IaC/migration role allowlist, or a deterministic equivalent), reject superuser/unapproved `BYPASSRLS`/credential material, and bind its hash to the dump manifest. Stream the logical dump over TLS directly to the protected GCS bucket with provider-side CMEK at rest, no persistent plaintext staging, and record the exact GCS `kmsKeyName`, referenced CryptoKeyVersion, and its provider-reported enabled state; hash the sanitized CMEK metadata receipt itself for integrity, never claim a CSEK-style key SHA; only the GCS service agent has the required KMS encrypt/decrypt binding, while backup/API/worker/dispatcher/reconciler identities have no direct KMS use permission. Require Todo 6's CMEK-lifecycle readback: every referenced version remains enabled with no scheduled disable/destruction for the full protected-object retention horizon, separate break-glass authority and alerts remain intact, and each rotation must restore an object encrypted with the prior version before the new version is accepted. Produce server-side generation-guarded copies of every governed immutable object byte from that same signed snapshot manifest. The manifest maps source bucket/key/generation/SHA-256 to backup key/generation/SHA-256 and records sanitized recovery-branch/project/LSN identities plus Auth/actor-link checksums. Replace an older rolling Final recovery branch only after the new branch/dump/object copies validate and retention/RPO policy permits it; then mark the exact publication `BACKUP_VALIDATED`, clear its next-Final/new-Run hold, and preserve any emergency-override incident until separately resolved. Restore only into an independently authorized recovery target provisioned/adopted through a verified dedicated Terraform `recovery` layer and disjoint remote-state prefix. Its immutable owner receipt binds: a distinct Neon recovery project, independently fingerprinted `EMPTY` root and fresh target branch; a target-scoped Neon Auth control-plane credential reference plus separate database-restore credential reference; a distinct recovery GCP project/bucket/prefix, restore-only service identity, UBLA, public-access prevention, CMEK mapping, empty fingerprint, and explicit `DISPOSABLE_PREVIEW` or `RETAINED_PRODUCTION` lifecycle/cleanup disposition. The primary broker and every API/worker/dispatcher/reconciler/backup identity are denied all target secrets and storage. Every restore command derives target project/branch/endpoint/bucket/prefix/identity only from this receipt. Before data mutation, record target `server_version_num`, exact restore image digest/platform and checksummed client hashes; strict v1 requires source server, target server and `psql`/`pg_dump`/`pg_dumpall`/`pg_restore` majors all equal. The selected v1 order is enable-Auth-before-data, but it is not inferred from generic database migration guidance: require a current owner/provider capability receipt that explicitly supports this order for the exact source/target Neon Auth provider and schema revisions and enumerates which provider-owned DDL/ownership is preserved and which allowlisted Auth data may be loaded; otherwise return `WAITING_FOR_OWNER` before target Auth or database mutation. Then use the isolated target Auth-control credential to enable Neon Auth on the empty target before `pg_restore`, capture target endpoint/config/schema revision/trusted-domain/JWKS readback with outbound email disabled, and prove the provider-created schema satisfies the empty-identity/data predicate while inventorying allowlisted bootstrap/configuration/JWKS rows and target-specific checksums. Restore freshly rotated allowlisted global roles first; restore application DDL/data normally, but exclude provider-owned target `neon_auth` DDL/ownership and load only capability-authorized portable Auth identity/organization/account/actor-link data into the already provisioned schema with an explicit target-ID/FK map while preserving target-specific configuration/JWKS and omitting source sessions after exact source/target Auth-schema compatibility passes. A schema mismatch is `WAITING_FOR_OWNER`, never a destructive fallback. After restore, prove zero active target sessions and reject any source/restored session, rotate target application session secrets, require re-auth/reset per Todo 14, read back Auth endpoint/config/JWKS/trusted domains plus actor-link checksums, and switch no references until database/Auth/object/CMEK verification is complete. The preview rehearsal must prove this exact cross-project sequence before production; generic `pg_dump` compatibility is not authority, the primary project's broker credential is never used against the target, and restore never points at production. Make each backup idempotent by a deterministic operation ID derived from `publication_event_id`; persist the broker operation before branch-create POST, use its deterministic recovery-branch name and immutable expected project/parent/LSN/timestamp/request-hash annotations, reconcile timeout/partial responses by exact-name lookup and verification, accept exactly one matching branch, and return `WAITING_FOR_OPERATOR` without acceptance/deletion on ambiguity. After entrypoint/tests pass, create the declared Todo 38 commit, require a clean committed image context, repeat Todo 36's locked build/SBOM/scan/push/digest wrapper for that commit, and guarded apply with `enable_backup_job=true`, rebinding the exact complete set `api,worker,dispatcher,recovery-broker,reconciler,backup,cutover-guardian,powerbi-session-broker,routing-compensation-broker,powerbi-session-executor` plus two private zone-disjoint session executor instances to that one digest before live rehearsal. Do not add a scheduler, expose the Neon key to the backup Job, overstate IAM as manifest/generation-scoped, claim method-scoped Neon API keys, claim delete denial as Bucket Lock proof, claim `pg_dump` alone covers Managed Auth, or claim daily RPO.
  Authoritative target-Auth bootstrap/restore protocol: The legacy whole-schema-emptiness premise is non-authoritative. Enabling Auth is expected to provision `neon_auth` and provider bootstrap configuration/JWKS state. Only after the Round 22 prestate classifies `ABSENT_EMPTY`, and before that path's enable-Auth POST, `configure_neon_restore_target_auth.py` freezes `<attemptDir>/task-38-target-auth-enable-intent.json` with target project/branch/database, source/target provider and schema revisions, capability-receipt SHA, requested config hash, and actor/context. `ACTIVE` is GET-only and creates no intent. `ABSENT_EMPTY` issues POST at most once; normal or uncertain completion must be reconciled with an Auth-details GET. Exactly one matching state may be adopted; absent, ambiguous, or mismatched readback returns `WAITING_FOR_OPERATOR` without POST retry or database restore. Its output must prove an empty-identity/data predicate: zero target user/account/session/organization/member/invitation/verification/application-actor records, while enumerating the provider-created bootstrap/configuration/JWKS rows allowed by the owner/provider capability receipt with table/key/count/checksum and target-specific ownership. The authoritative command uses `--require-empty-auth-identity-data --allow-provider-bootstrap-rows-from-capability-receipt --record-bootstrap-row-allowlist-and-checksums --preserve-target-provider-config-jwks --operation-intent <attemptDir>/task-38-target-auth-enable-intent.json --post-at-most-once --reconcile-auth-details-get --no-blind-post-retry`. Restore excludes provider-owned target DDL/ownership and never overlays source configuration/JWKS; it loads only the capability receipt's portable, schema-compatible identity/organization/account/actor-link data, applies its explicit target-ID/FK mapping, rejects unexpected bootstrap rows/conflicts, records source-versus-target config/JWKS checksums as non-restored mapping evidence, omits source session rows and proves zero active target sessions, then rotates application secrets and requires re-auth/reset. Preview QA injects POST timeout/response loss/crash, absent/ambiguous/mismatched/matching GET readback, nonempty identity data, unallowlisted bootstrap row, config/JWKS overwrite, mapping conflict, and source session load; only the exact matching adoption plus allowlisted merge can proceed.

Immediately before the Groups API grant, inside the manifest-owned grant process, run one fenced Neon transaction that compare-and-sets the exact operation from `ARMED` to `GRANT_STARTING`, writes `grant_call_started_at = clock_timestamp()`, `grant_start_txid = txid_current()`, `grant_start_lsn = pg_current_wal_lsn()`, and an opaque request nonce, commits, then dispatches the reviewed Groups API request through a calibrated wrapper within at most two monotonic seconds; the provider request ID/readback and wrapper monotonic delta are appended to the same operation journal. Immediately before the guardian leader's first compensation provider call, it runs the analogous fenced compare-and-set to `COMPENSATION_STARTING`, writes `compensation_call_started_at = clock_timestamp()`, `compensation_start_txid`, `compensation_start_lsn`, and its request nonce in the same Neon row, commits, and dispatches through the same at-most-two-second calibrated wrapper. The database constraint and terminal validator require `compensation_call_started_at <= grant_call_started_at + interval '900 seconds'`, the same release/attempt/fence, monotonically ordered LSN/transaction evidence, both wrapper deltas at most two seconds, and matching provider request/readback nonces. A server-time query without the fenced write, a host timestamp, missing/late provider dispatch, cross-operation timestamp, Neon clock/LSN uncertainty, or compensation start after the bound cannot produce `ROLLED_BACK_SAFE`; Cloud Tasks may only wake a retry and never supplies or extends either timestamp.

The preassigned `PSIViewer` membership is inert without workspace Read and may be removed later only by an owner-receipted portal action, while the consumer group is never in an app audience. `finalize` is the sole availability switch: after D-F pass, it verifies the immutable owner receipt for the exact QA-only app revision/audience and the already assigned consumer `PSIViewer` role, uses only the supported Groups API to add the approved consumer group as workspace Viewer and read that requested grant back, enters `PENDING_ACCESS_PROPAGATION`, refreshes each consumer canary's permissions at most once per hour, waits at least two minutes, discards prior tokens/cookies, and requires isolated fresh-sign-in Viewer+RLS positive proof plus nonmember/cross-team negative proof. It never publishes/updates an app, changes app audiences, or writes RLS through API/XMLA. A partial finalize immediately triggers the fenced access-only guardian, which removes consumer workspace Viewer first, disables traffic, signs out, proves isolated fresh-session denial, retains the reviewed deployment private, and leaves QA access removed; it never restores or rebinds infrastructure, data, Auth, Power BI configuration, or DNS. A post-finalize rollback requires a separate incident receipt and uses the same Viewer-first ordering. Empty-path locked objects remain quarantined/unreferenced, and after the first protected Final plus complete backup validates, the empty baseline is retired in favor of a reviewed `EXISTING_DATA` recovery manifest. Never create any non-state production resource or retention lock before the A0 recovery receipt passes, attempt backup before A1 foundation, migrate during foundation, reuse a preview QA bridge as production evidence, grant consumer workspace Viewer before `finalize`, put the consumer group in any app audience, publish an app outside the Phase-D owner receipt, or mutate RLS/app state through unsupported APIs, use pilot as authority, accept lane-modified index, or alter reviewed source.
  Production state invariant: the authorization receipt must name the approved state bucket, disjoint layer prefixes, minimum 90-day superseded-generation recovery objective, day-90 deletion eligibility, explicit soft-delete duration, current and retained-generation CMEK mappings, separate disable/destroy authority, and alerts. A0 cannot complete and A1 cannot start until a provider-backed rehearsal rotates the state key and decrypts/restores one older state generation and proves its mapped CMEK remains usable until provider-confirmed irrecoverability; the mutable current state object remains writable under backend locking and is never Bucket-Locked. Every A0/A1/later Terraform mutation uses the encrypted ephemeral wrapper; ordinary success leaves no local state, while any backend-persist fallback immediately stops mutation, enters separate-authority encrypted quarantine/lineage/serial/remote-generation reconciliation, and blocks the next phase until non-forced remote recovery is read back and residue is zero. A0's plan/apply allowlist contains only the state bucket, state KMS/IAM/audit/locking prerequisites and sanitized probe generations; any API, Artifact Registry, application bucket, retention lock, queue, secret, workload, domain, or Power BI mutation before the frozen A0 recovery receipt is a hard failure.

Timeout, response loss, or crash never causes a blind retry: exactly one matching state is adopted, while absent, ambiguous, or mismatched readback returns `WAITING_FOR_OPERATOR` with public routing and Power BI consumer access disabled. The authoritative production configure invocation appends `--operation-intent <attemptDir>/task-41-production-auth-enable-intent.json --post-at-most-once --reconcile-auth-details-get --no-blind-post-retry`; Phase-B/Phase-C tests cover normal confirmation, matching adoption, absent/multiple/mismatched readback, target bootstrap allowlist drift, source config/JWKS overwrite, portable-data mapping conflict, source-session load, and any downstream action before reconciled Auth-live evidence.

Every Phase A1-through-rollback producer consumes the immutable production state-recovery receipt and propagates its SHA. Every path consumes a production-only pre-backup Auth inventory bound to the exact production project/branch/endpoint and rejects preview authority. `EXISTING_DATA` Phase B additionally consumes a comprehensive remotely state-backed restore-target receipt covering distinct Neon project/empty root/fresh branch, target Auth-control/database secrets, recovery GCP project/bucket/prefix, restore identity, UBLA/public denial/CMEK/empty fingerprint/lifecycle disposition and a production-specific PostgreSQL client/server/export-role capability receipt, and proves a broker-verified recovery branch, child-only admin lifecycle, export-only credential denied on primary, common snapshot, password-free allowlisted global-role manifest, protected objects, and restore into that independently empty target with source/target/client major equality, reviewed image/toolchain identity, target Auth enabled before `pg_restore`, provider-owned Auth DDL preserved, schema-compatible allowlisted Auth data restored, sessions revoked/secrets rotated and target Auth endpoint/config/JWKS/trusted-domain/actor-link readback; `EMPTY_FIRST_DEPLOYMENT` proves a signed waiver plus independent empty fingerprints. Migration, production Auth config/live, app, production QA bridge, and direct production-data-state receipts are ordered, production-specific, UPN-free, and cannot be replaced by preview evidence. Domain/TLS proves ownership, exact hostname, active certificate, valid chain/SNI/HTTPS, QA-only reviewed digest, denied public backend, and restorable prior routing. Power BI Phase D proves the exact shared-capacity refresh budget/reservation/coalescing/reset/deadline or owner-approved capacity upgrade, permission propagation through rate-aware refresh/wait/discarded sessions/fresh sign-ins, owner-portal binding, consumer-group `PSIViewer` membership while the group has no workspace Viewer/Read, QA validation-group Viewer+`PSIViewer`, exactly two QA identities, temporary Build removal, exact refresh/reconciliation, and an owner-published QA-only app whose audience excludes the consumer group; REST app mutation and XMLA RLS writes are absent. Rollback-ready freezes all recovery inputs plus the access-only partial-finalize compensation operation. Finalize treats Groups readback as requested access only and enables public TLS routing only after the named consumer canaries prove effective Viewer+RLS access with fresh sign-ins; then it removes temporary QA access. Partial-finalize compensation requests consumer workspace Viewer removal first through the private session executors, disables and reads back the exact public route through the scoped routing broker, signs out, and proves propagated fresh-session denial for every exact member; its only safe result is access revoked with the reviewed deployment retained private and it never claims image/database/Auth/object/final-pointer/Power BI binding/RLS/app/DNS restoration. ## Final verification wave

Authoritative F3 child/validation protocol: substitute the later shorter F3 payload with exactly `spawn_agent({"task_name":"f3_manual_qa","fork_turns":"none","message":"DECLARED_ROLE=lazycodex-qa-executor.
